# Proposed OEIS comments: A212941 to A222001

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A212941

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A212941>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [2002](../papers/2001-2500/2002-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 130453*a(n-1) -6763333836*a(n-2) +185208872760324*a(n-3) -2991440828618168976*a(n-4) +29966228467174160130816*a(n-5) -189618846625740940674481152*a(n-6) +754213725094693350993932353536*a(n-7) -1831929539263722316502211827073024*a(n-8) +2545588680910314328406052486644760576*a(n-9) -1758206800538810904218951296840095498240*a(n-10) +418951577236630476027552063815969641857024*a(n-11).
> Empirical: a(n) = 6*15876^n -3*4536^n +2*1512^n +3136^n -5*27216^n -462^n -6*7056^n -9261^n +4*12096^n +2*2646^n +46656^n. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 27 2012

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. "In all rows" quantifies over the whole array, so no bounded window decides it, but it is a conjunction of independent per-row facts: keep one bit per adjacent column pair, recording whether every row so far has put column j above column j-1. A row can only clear bits, and the array is admissible exactly when all are clear at the end. So the state is that bit mask alone, giving 11 states, however large the alphabet, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 11. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A212942

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A212942>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [1798](../papers/1501-2000/1798-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2323483*a(n-1) -2167154969952*a(n-2) +1086330796113513244*a(n-3) -329778884328286978977952*a(n-4) +64517502456132437770369148544*a(n-5) -8421554343462670052897532765935616*a(n-6) +747012180292693168982253383087616310272*a(n-7) -45320700037087646211097507312328132762984448*a(n-8) +1872991150391269654429084597380378344674028617728*a(n-9) -51936059702018704067955656273063090467733238547218432*a(n-10) +938955828089973458590692996057468119367652949010608553984*a(n-11) -10544878359956363252917775501846837273628118104421027150823424*a(n-12) +67581624519887879771651501946182123485150605725959917286635601920*a(n-13) -209181393867491964480827236276816398998810506947956308433062959513600*a(n-14) +205819420752306963772376008790571963882492713708055771807588273881088000*a(n-15)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. "In all rows" quantifies over the whole array, so no bounded window decides it, but it is a conjunction of independent per-row facts: keep one bit per adjacent column pair, recording whether every row so far has put column j above column j-1. A row can only clear bits, and the array is admissible exactly when all are clear at the end. So the state is that bit mask alone, giving 15 states, however large the alphabet, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 15. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A213203

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A213203>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [9240](../papers/9001-9500/9240-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) + (-n^2-n-11)*a(n-1) + (n^3+7*n^2-13*n+39)*a(n-2) - 2*(n-2)*(4*n^2-2*n-15)*a(n-3) + 20*(n-2)*(n-3)*(n-4)*a(n-4) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 21 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A214098

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A214098>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [2997](../papers/2501-3000/2997-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 19*a(n-1) - 71*a(n-2) + 86*a(n-3) - 24*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(11 - 66*x + 105*x^2 - 32*x^3) / ((1 - 2*x)*(1 - 17*x + 37*x^2 - 12*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Jul 22 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 68 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A215973

```

## A217358

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A217358>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [9921](../papers/9501-10000/9921-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 46*n*(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n) -(n-1)*(n-2)*(11*n-74)*a(n-1) -(n-2)*(336*n^2-1359*n+1351)*a(n-2) +(-347*n^3+2190*n^2-3861*n+1330)*a(n-3) + 8*(2*n-7)*(4*n-15)*(4*n-17)*a(n-4) = 0.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

## A218348

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A218348>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2833](../papers/2501-3000/2833-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 21 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A219314

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*a(n) -2*n*a(n-1) +11*(-n+2)*a(n-2) +4*(2*n-5)*a(n-3) +8*(5*n-17)*a(n-4) +24*(n-4)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016
> Conjecture: n*(5*n-7)*a(n) -4*(5*n^2-12*n+6)*a(n-1) -(15*n^2-11*n-30) *a(n-2) +2*(35*n^2-119*n+66)*a(n-3) +12*(n-3)*(5*n2)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A220563

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A220563>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3123](../papers/3001-3500/3123-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 5 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [567](../papers/0501-1000/0567-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 36*a(n-2) -200*a(n-4) -40*a(n-6) -84*a(n-8) +80*a(n-10) -20*a(n-12) +80*a(n-14) for n>20

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 481 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 20. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
